<a id='question-150'></a>

### Question 150: Drug Interaction Clinical Vignette 50: SLCO1B1 Polymorphisms: OATP1B1 Transporter Defect & Statin Myopathy Susceptibility
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Genetic polymorphisms affecting drug transporters (specifically SLCO1B1 encoding OATP1B1) can significantly alter drug pharmacokinetics and increase the risk of adverse drug reactions, such as statin-induced myopathy. (基因多态性影响药物转运蛋白 (特别是 SLCO1B1 编码的 OATP1B1) 会显著改变药物药代动力学，并增加不良药物反应的风险，例如他汀类药物引起的肌病。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 58-year-old Asian American male with a history of hyperlipidemia is started on atorvastatin 80 mg daily. He has well-controlled hypertension managed with lisinopril and a history of type 2 diabetes mellitus treated with metformin. Three weeks after initiating atorvastatin, he presents to the emergency department complaining of severe, diffuse muscle pain, weakness, and dark urine. His creatine kinase (CK) level is markedly elevated at 15,000 U/L (normal range: 20-200 U/L). Urinalysis shows myoglobinuria. Renal function tests (BUN, creatinine) are within normal limits. The patient denies any recent strenuous exercise or trauma. He reports no other new medications or supplements. Genetic testing reveals he is homozygous for the SLCO1B1 c.3605G>A variant (rs4385143). Which of the following mechanisms best explains this patient's adverse reaction to atorvastatin?

(A) Increased hepatic uptake of atorvastatin due to enhanced OATP1B1 transporter activity, leading to higher intracellular concentrations and increased risk of toxicity.
(B) Decreased hepatic uptake of atorvastatin due to impaired OATP1B1 transporter function, resulting in increased systemic exposure and accumulation in muscle tissue.
(C) Increased biliary excretion of atorvastatin metabolites due to enhanced OATP1B1 activity, leading to reduced enterohepatic recirculation and decreased overall drug exposure.
(D) Decreased renal excretion of atorvastatin due to impaired OATP1B1 function in the proximal tubules, leading to increased systemic exposure.
(E) Increased metabolism of atorvastatin by CYP3A4 due to induction by the SLCO1B1 variant, leading to increased formation of toxic metabolites.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 58-year-old Asian American male with hyperlipidemia, hypertension, and type 2 diabetes. Asian ethnicity is a known risk factor for certain genetic polymorphisms.
- **Medication:** Started on atorvastatin 80 mg (a high dose).
- **Presentation:** Severe muscle pain, weakness, dark urine (suggestive of rhabdomyolysis), markedly elevated CK (15,000 U/L), myoglobinuria. This clinical picture strongly indicates statin-induced myopathy/rhabdomyolysis.
- **Genetic Finding:** Homozygous for the SLCO1B1 c.3605G>A variant (rs4385143). This specific variant is well-known to be associated with reduced function of the OATP1B1 transporter.
- **Question:** Asks for the mechanism explaining the adverse reaction.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **OATP1B1 (Organic Anion Transporting Polypeptide 1B1):** This is a hepatic uptake transporter encoded by the SLCO1B1 gene. It is located on the sinusoidal membrane of hepatocytes and is responsible for transporting various organic anions, including statins (like atorvastatin), into the liver.
- **SLCO1B1 Polymorphisms:** Certain variants (like c.3605G>A, rs4385143) lead to reduced function or expression of the OATP1B1 transporter.
- **Impact on Atorvastatin:** Atorvastatin is a substrate for OATP1B1. Reduced OATP1B1 function impairs the hepatic uptake of atorvastatin from the blood into the liver.
- **Consequence:** Impaired hepatic uptake leads to decreased first-pass metabolism and increased systemic exposure to atorvastatin. Higher concentrations of atorvastatin reach peripheral tissues, including skeletal muscle.
- **Mechanism of Statin Myopathy:** Statins can cause muscle toxicity (myopathy, myositis, rhabdomyolysis). The exact mechanism is complex but involves mitochondrial dysfunction, oxidative stress, and impaired protein synthesis in muscle cells. Higher drug concentrations in muscle increase the risk and severity of these effects.
- **Connecting the Dots:** The patient has a genetic variant known to impair OATP1B1 function. This impairment reduces atorvastatin uptake into the liver, increases systemic exposure, and leads to higher concentrations in muscle, precipitating severe myopathy/rhabdomyolysis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. The SLCO1B1 variant *decreases* OATP1B1 function, not enhances it. Increased uptake would lead to *lower* systemic exposure, not higher.
- (C): Incorrect. OATP1B1 is primarily involved in hepatic *uptake*, not biliary excretion. While biliary excretion is a route for some drugs, it's not the primary mechanism affected by OATP1B1 dysfunction in this context, and reduced enterohepatic recirculation would *decrease* overall exposure, not increase it.
- (D): Incorrect. OATP1B1 is primarily a *hepatic* transporter. While OATP family members exist in the kidney, OATP1B1's main role is in the liver. Furthermore, statins are primarily metabolized in the liver and excreted via bile, not significantly via renal excretion mediated by OATP1B1. Impaired renal excretion would not be the primary mechanism leading to increased systemic exposure and muscle toxicity.
- (E): Incorrect. The SLCO1B1 variant affects a *transporter*, not directly CYP3A4 enzyme activity. While CYP3A4 metabolizes atorvastatin, the primary effect of the SLCO1B1 variant is on uptake, not metabolism via CYP3A4 induction. Induction would lead to *faster* metabolism and *lower* drug levels.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Correct Mechanism (B)                                   | Distractor (A)                                         | Distractor (C)                                         | Distractor (D)                                         | Distractor (E)                                         |
|----------------------|---------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------|
| **OATP1B1 Function** | Decreased                                               | Increased (Incorrect)                                   | Enhanced (Incorrect)                                   | Impaired (Incorrect Location/Function)                 | Unaffected (Incorrect Mechanism)                        |
| **Hepatic Uptake**   | Decreased                                               | Increased (Incorrect)                                   | Unrelated (Biliary Excretion)                           | Unrelated (Renal Excretion)                             | Unrelated (CYP3A4 Metabolism)                           |
| **Systemic Exposure**| Increased                                               | Decreased (Incorrect)                                   | Decreased (Incorrect)                                   | Increased (Incorrect Mechanism)                         | Decreased (Incorrect Mechanism)                         |
| **Muscle Exposure**  | Increased                                               | Decreased (Incorrect)                                   | Decreased (Incorrect)                                   | Increased (Incorrect Mechanism)                         | Decreased (Incorrect Mechanism)                         |
| **Outcome**          | Statin Myopathy/Rhabdomyolysis (Correct)                | Reduced Toxicity (Incorrect)                            | Reduced Toxicity (Incorrect)                            | Increased Toxicity (Incorrect Mechanism)                 | Increased Toxicity (Incorrect Mechanism)                 |
| **Genetic Basis**    | SLCO1B1 loss-of-function variant (e.g., c.3605G>A)     | SLCO1B1 gain-of-function variant (Hypothetical)         | SLCO1B1 gain-of-function variant (Hypothetical)         | SLCO1B1 loss-of-function variant (Incorrect Location) | SLCO1B1 variant affecting CYP3A4 (Incorrect)           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Pharmacogenetic variations in drug transporters, such as SLCO1B1 encoding OATP1B1, can significantly impact drug disposition and toxicity. Reduced OATP1B1 function impairs hepatic uptake of statins, increasing systemic exposure and the risk of myopathy. Key differentials for statin myopathy include hypothyroidism, electrolyte abnormalities, and drug interactions (e.g., fibrates, certain antifungals).
